### Row #L48 - The Data explorer's line under an answer ends on the last day it read

- **Scope:** The line under a Data explorer answer, "Read from {n} UTC days, {first} to {last}.", ends on the last day the explorer read, not on the window's last day, and counts the days up to that day. Level 2.
- **The fault** (row L46's report, 2026-10-08; plan 55's owner, 2026-10-08; the line's source read on `origin/main` at 4ed116b2f): row L12 (#1364) wrote the line. It takes its last day from the window, not from the days the explorer read: `+page.svelte` hands `describeDaysRead` the window's last day, `runSpan.to`, and an `ok` answer names only the first day it read, `readFrom`. On a build before L46 (#1427) the line named 8 Oct while the explorer read only through 6 Oct. Since L46 it holds on the committed data only because every ledger's raw listing reaches 8 Oct. A ledger's newest listed day is the later of the newest day its indexes name and the newest raw day the build listed for it, its `through` in `frontend/src/lib/data/ask-reader.ts`. So whenever every chosen ledger's newest listed day is before the window's last day, the line names a day the explorer never read.
- **Files touched** (found by a search on `origin/main` at 4ed116b2f for the line's words "Read from", `describeDaysRead`, `days-read`, `AnswerTable`, `spanText`, `readFrom` and `firstDayRead` under `frontend/`, `docs/`, `backend/` and `.github/`; searched again at dispatch on `origin/main` at 11adb93e3, and ec7923862 changes no file under `frontend/`: no other file, and the other hits, `readFrom` in `MemoryBoard.svelte`, `machine.ts` and `console-memory-board.spec.ts` and `spanText` in `Viewport.svelte`, are fields of their own):
  - `frontend/src/lib/console/explorer/days-read.ts` (`describeDaysRead(from, to)` writes the line and counts its days; found during execution: only its comment changes, because `to` is now the last day the answer read)
  - `frontend/src/routes/console/data-explorer/+page.svelte` (hands `describeDaysRead` the answer's `readFrom` and `runSpan.to`, the window's last day; it already holds `cost.through`, how far each chosen ledger reaches, from the estimate before a run; held by plan 55's row "The reader chooses the chart and the columns it draws", decision 3; found during execution: it hands the answer's `readFrom` and `readTo`)
  - `frontend/tests/console-data-explorer.spec.ts` (the Oracle, beside L12's "THE ORACLE: an answer over two ledgers that began on different days names each with its own first day, counts the days it read, and keeps that line when the window moves before the next Run"; it pins the line; held by plan 55's row "The reader chooses the chart and the columns it draws")
  - `frontend/tests/ledger-lifecycle.spec.ts` (pins the line in the reader's cases: `daysReadLines` hands `describeDaysRead` a last day each case passes in, as the page hands it the window's last day; found during execution: `daysReadLines` hands it the answer's own `readTo`, each `ok` case names `readTo`, and four cases are new, decision 7)
  - `frontend/tests/explorer-boundary.spec.ts` (pins the line in L1's browser case; found during execution: no change, because its ledger reaches the window's last day, so its line holds)
  - `docs/architecture/publishing/how-the-query-door-answers-a-written-question.md` (says the line's last day is the window's)
  - `docs/how-to/query-a-ledger-from-the-console.md` (quotes the line and says which days it counts)
  - `frontend/src/lib/data/ask-reader.ts` and `frontend/src/lib/data/slice-shapes.ts`, only if the answer must say which day it read through (decision 5): `readAsk` names `readFrom` through `firstDayRead`, each ledger's plan holds its `through`, and the `ok` answer of `AskResult` carries no last day. Found during execution: both change (decision 5)
  - Read, no change: `frontend/src/lib/console/explorer/AnswerTable.svelte`, which prints the line it is handed, `spanText`, in its `.answer-note`
- **Acceptance gates:** local: `npm --prefix frontend run test:changed -- --spec console-data-explorer.spec.ts --spec ledger-lifecycle.spec.ts --spec explorer-boundary.spec.ts`; `npm --prefix frontend run check`; `doc_load.py` on the two pages; the browser smoke of the data explorer. CI: the pull request runs the console specs; every group runs on the merge push.
- **Oracle:** in `console-data-explorer.spec.ts`, beside L12's oracle, on ledgers the test builds (Table D, D3), with a window that ends after one ledger's newest listed day: chosen alone, the line names that ledger's newest listed day as its last day and counts the days up to it; chosen beside a second built ledger that reaches the window's last day, the line names the window's last day. On `origin/main` the first case names the window's last day, which is what lets this check fail; the second passes on both, and fails a fix that takes the earlier of the two ledgers' last days. It cannot settle a newest listed day that only a raw listing names, because `serveToPage` in `frontend/tests/support/ledger-lifecycle.ts` serves packed days and bakes no raw listing.

**Decisions**

| # | Decision | Authority |
| --- | --- | --- |
| 1 | Plan 62 takes the row, because its row L12 wrote the line | Plan owner, 2026-10-08; plan 55's owner agreed, 2026-10-08 |
| 2 | Reader chooses the words if the sentence changes form (CLAUDE.md section 14). Not needed: the sentence keeps its form, and only its last day and its count change | Plan owner, 2026-10-08 |
| 3 | L48 waits for plan 55's row "The reader chooses the chart and the columns it draws", which edits `+page.svelte` and `console-data-explorer.spec.ts` but not `days-read.ts` or `AnswerTable.svelte`. A change in `days-read.ts` alone could go now, but it cannot fix the line: `+page.svelte` hands it the window's last day, and an `ok` answer names no last day read, so the fix changes `+page.svelte` | Plan 55's owner, 2026-10-08 (what waits); plan author, 2026-10-08 (the search) |
| 4 | If the fix needs the data layer under `frontend/src/lib/data/`, `ledger.ts`, `ask-reader.ts`, `slice-shapes.ts` or `page-keeper.ts`, to say which days an answer read, the row's worker tells plan 55's owner before starting. Told on 2026-10-08, before the change was built: `ask-reader.ts` and `slice-shapes.ts` change, and in `+page.svelte` only the line's expression. Plan 55's owner agreed: no plan 55 row in flight edits either file, and its row "The chart keeps its room" edits other parts of `+page.svelte` | Plan 55's owner, 2026-10-08 |
| 5 | The answer carries the last day it read, from the plan it ran. An `ok` answer gains `readTo` beside `readFrom`, which `lastDayRead` works out beside `firstDayRead` in `ask-reader.ts`, on the plans the answer ran, after any plan made again when the repository fails. One rule: a ledger reads from its start day, its cut day, else the day the repository could not give days before, else the window's first day, to the earlier of the window's last day and its `through`; a ledger whose start is after that end read no day and names none, so a ledger that began after the window, or whose site days start after it while the repository cannot answer, never moves the end. `readTo`, not `readThrough`: `through` already means how far a ledger reaches. Two commits: the start day moves out of `firstDayRead` into a function of its own, with no change in behaviour, then the behaviour. `AskResult` is never persisted, so Table C, C1 does not fire | Fowler, 2026-10-08 |
| 6 | Level 2: the line under every explorer answer changes, three specs pin it and two pages describe it, and the fix may change `readAsk` in `ask-reader.ts`, through which every explorer question reads | Plan author, 2026-10-08 |
| 7 | The reader's cases in `ledger-lifecycle.spec.ts` run the real engine on ledgers they build, so they are integration tier. `daysReadLines` hands `describeDaysRead` the answer's own `readTo` and works out nothing itself, so it tests `readAsk` and not a rule of its own. Four new cases: both Oracle cases, so a fix that takes the earlier of two last days fails without a browser; a ledger that began after the window's last day, and a ledger the repository cannot answer for whose site days start after the window, each beside a ledger that stops before the window's last day. No case for a newest day only a raw listing names: the rule reads `through` the same way whichever tier set it | Fowler, 2026-10-08 |

| 2 | Fix it in L46 | L46's fault was the bake, and the line held on the data L46 smoked: every ledger's raw listing reached 8 Oct, the window's last day | L46 would have changed `+page.svelte` too, and its bake fix would have waited for plan 55's row "The reader chooses the chart and the columns it draws" | Plan owner, 2026-10-08 |
| 3 | The page takes the last day from `cost.through`, the estimate before a run, and keeps it with the run | The estimate is worked out at another moment, and maybe for other ledgers: ticking a ledger starts it and nothing waits for it, Run included, so a Run pressed just after a tick can come first, and the line is right only most of the time (Guardrail #5) | No change under `frontend/src/lib/data/`; a copy of the rule in the page, apart from `firstDayRead`; and either that race or a Run that waits for the estimate, which also waits for the column rail's file | Fowler, 2026-10-08 |

### Row #L49 - Every console route asks search engines not to list it

- **Scope:** Every console route carries `<meta name="robots" content="noindex" />` in its head, so a search engine that crawls it does not list it. Level to be set from the search below.
- **The gap** (row L43's report, finding 1, 2026-10-08): a search of `frontend/src/routes/console/` for `noindex` finds it on three routes, Pipelines, Hardware and Summaries, and finds it on none of Judgement, Voices and Data explorer; the site ships no `frontend/static/robots.txt`. A search engine that crawls the console may list those three routes.
- **Why CI did not catch it:** `.github/workflows/ci.yml`'s `robots` job runs `pytest backend/tests/test_extract.py`, which checks the article extractor's own RFC 9309 `robots.txt` parsing for sources this project crawls. It reads nothing under `frontend/src/routes/console/` and asserts no console-route meta tag, so a console route with no `noindex` tag passes that job every time.
- **Files touched** (found by a search on `origin/main` at 3ba1b39b8 for `noindex` under `frontend/src/routes/console/`, for `robots` under `.github/workflows/` and `backend/tests/`, and for `robots.txt` under `frontend/static/`; search again at dispatch):
  - `frontend/src/routes/console/judgement/+page.svelte` (sets no `noindex`; held by L38)
  - `frontend/src/routes/console/voices/+page.svelte` (sets no `noindex`)
  - `frontend/src/routes/console/data-explorer/+page.svelte` (sets no `noindex`; held by plan 55's row "The reader chooses the chart and the columns it draws")
  - `frontend/src/routes/console/+layout.svelte` (a candidate home for one shared tag, if Fowler rules for it; L42 changes this file for the days control, so coordinate before dispatch)
  - `frontend/src/routes/console/+page.svelte`, `frontend/src/routes/console/machine/+page.svelte`, `frontend/src/routes/console/model/+page.svelte` (already set `noindex`; read, to confirm the pattern a shared or per-page fix follows)
  - `.github/workflows/ci.yml` (the `robots` job; read, to say why it did not catch this)
- **Acceptance gates:** local: `npm --prefix frontend run test:changed -- --list` for the affected specs; `npm --prefix frontend run check`; the browser smoke of each console route, reading its rendered head. CI: the pull request runs the console specs; every group runs on the merge push.
- **Oracle:** on a page the test builds, or on the canary page: every console route's rendered head carries `<meta name="robots" content="noindex" />`. On `origin/main` Judgement, Voices and Data explorer carry no such tag, which is what lets this check fail.

**Decisions**

| # | Decision | Authority |
| --- | --- | --- |
| 1 | Fowler rules the shape: one tag in a shared place such as `console/+layout.svelte`, or one tag on each page | To be ruled at dispatch (Fowler) |
| 2 | Level set from the search: a missing tag on three pages, fixed by one line each or by one shared line, following a pattern three other pages already set; a wrong version is obvious and local | Plan owner, 2026-10-08 |

**Rejected alternatives**

| # | Option | Why rejected | What it would cost to take | Authority |
| --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |

### Row #L50 - Two console tests prove what they claim

- **Scope:** Two console test assertions are fixed to catch the fault each claims to catch. Level 1.
- **Finding 2** (row L43's report, 2026-10-08): `frontend/tests/console-chart-lifetime.spec.ts`, near line 357, asserts `performance.getEntriesByType('navigation').length === 1` to prove a tab click did not reload the page. Measured with Playwright 1.62.1 on Chromium, that count is also 1 after a full page load; the spec's own comment says a full page load "resets it and proves nothing," yet the assertion cannot tell the two apart, because it reads the same value either way. `performance.timeOrigin` does: a reload sets a new origin, where an in-page navigation keeps the one the first load set.
- **Finding 3** (row L43's report, 2026-10-08): `frontend/tests/console-nav.spec.ts`'s strip-reading helper, `tabs(page)`, is called straight after `page.goto` with no wait for the strip's own tabs to appear. On the client-drawn Data explorer route, on a busy machine, the read once found no tabs; a re-run passed. Waiting for the strip's first tab before reading it closes the race.
- **Files touched** (found by a search on `origin/main` at 3ba1b39b8; search again at dispatch):
  - `frontend/tests/console-chart-lifetime.spec.ts` (the "no reload" assertion near line 357; the Oracle for finding 2)
  - `frontend/tests/console-nav.spec.ts` (the `tabs(page)` helper and its callers; the Oracle for finding 3)
- **Acceptance gates:** local: `npm --prefix frontend run test:changed -- --spec console-chart-lifetime.spec.ts --spec console-nav.spec.ts`; `npm --prefix frontend run check`. CI: the pull request runs the console specs; every group runs on the merge push.
- **Oracle:** (a) the "no reload" check fails when the test is changed to force a full page load on purpose, which it cannot do today. (b) `tabs(page)` waits for the strip's first tab to appear before it reads the strip, so a slow-drawn strip no longer reads as empty.

**Decisions**

| # | Decision | Authority |
| --- | --- | --- |
| 1 | Level 1: two test assertions, each read in one file, each fixed by comparing against a value the test can observe or by adding one wait | Plan owner, 2026-10-08 |
| 2 | The forced full page load and the held-back band file are shown in the row's report and are not kept as standing cases. The first would check only the browser's rule that a new document takes a new time origin, which no edit to the console can break. The second would add a slow-network case to the strip's checks, which this row does not ask for | This row's worker, on the plan owner's dispatch note, 2026-10-08 (found during execution) |
| 3 | The strip read waits with `expect(...).toBeAttached()`, not `locator.waitFor()`. A strip that never draws then fails inside the suite's 15-second expect timeout, with a message that names the route. `waitFor` has no limit of its own in this suite, so it would hold the test to its 180-second limit | This row's worker, 2026-10-08 (found during execution) |

**Rejected alternatives**

| # | Option | Why rejected | What it would cost to take | Authority |
| --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |
| 1 | As today | The "no reload" check cannot fail, so a regression that reloads the page on a tab click ships unnoticed; the strip read can flake on a busy machine and hide a real drawing fault behind a re-run | Nothing to build, and two checks that do not catch what they claim to | Row L43's report, findings 2 and 3 |
| 2 | Keep the forced full page load and the held-back band file as standing cases | Decision 2 | Two more cases in every console run, one of them a load of the Hardware route | Found during execution |

### Row #L51 - Judgement's agreement panel words follow the rates they judge

- **Scope:** Judgement's agreement panel reads right in every case Reader named, the panel's note uses one word for a dashed line and one word for each of a chart line and a run's progress, and a disagreed share that rounds to 0% but is not zero prints under the console's `<1` rule. Level 1.

Its two callers, for 1 of 300 pairs: the strip's "disagreed" entry and the dots' name (`readingsOf`) printed `0% of 300 pairs` and now print `<1% of 300 pairs`; the window sentence (`disagreeShare`) printed `In this one day, 0% of 300 pairs disagreed with their own second reading, ...` and now prints `In this one day, <1% of 300 pairs disagreed with their own second reading, ...` | Plan owner, 2026-10-08; found during execution |
| 4 | A share is past its mark when it is above it, the run's own test (`gates` in `backend/idhazh/similarity/fit.py`), and the verdict is on the window's two shares, as Reader's words say. The sentence for days held because of the judge does not change | Found during execution |
| 5 | The Oracle: in `console-window.spec.ts`, on days the test builds (Table D, D3), four cases join the agreement strip's cases, each checking the strip's heading and two entries, every drawn day's dots' name and the sentence, written out whole: at 7 days, Reader's own example, 9 of 45 pairs disagreed and 1 of the 36 that agreed could not tell, on days held because the record was too small; at 1 day, 15 of the 38 that agreed could not tell; at 7 days, both shares past their marks; at 1 day, 1 of 300 pairs disagreed. One case a window, at 1 and 7 days, reads the note and the two plot labels whole. A logic test of the `<1` rule joins `merge-line.spec.ts` | Found during execution (Table D, D3) |

**Rejected alternatives**

| # | Option | Why rejected | What it would cost to take | Authority |

### Row #L52 - Judgement names the line each build recorded

- **Scope:** Judgement's merge line, verdict split and holdout margin name the line from the run record of the build that made the day, where the record holds it, instead of the line the fitted rows' rule works out. Level 3.
- **The fault** (row L38's report, finding 1, recorded in L38's section, 2026-10-08): the rule the merge line, the verdict split and the holdout margin share, `findAppliedLine` in `frontend/src/lib/console/applied-line.ts`, works out the line a build used from the fitted rows. It can name a line no build used. The council's run, scheduled at 22:00 UTC on day D (`.github/workflows/llm-council.yml`), files the row dated D-1 after D's builds ran (`.github/workflows/digest.yml`), so a site built again before D+1's first publish names that row's line for D. Pages builds `main`'s tip again after each push to `main` whose CI passed and that changed a file the site is built from (`.github/workflows/pages.yml`). The run record already holds the line each build grouped stories at, `same_story_floor_applied` on each `RunRecord` of `RunManifest.runs` (`backend/idhazh/contracts/run_manifest.py`), in the day's `frontend/public/digest/<YYYY>/<MM>/<DD>/run.json`, and the console reads none of it. Nothing shows wrong while the merge switch is off, because every build then groups at the committed floor, and the rule names that floor too.
- **Files touched** (found by a search on `origin/main` at 96249a64c for `same_story_floor_applied`, `RunManifest`, `run.json`, `loadManifests`, `findAppliedLine` and `adaptive_dedup_threshold` under `frontend/`, `backend/`, `docs/`, `config/` and `.github/`; searched again at dispatch, on `origin/main` at 173f3f32a, which found the same files and no other reader of `same_story_floor_applied` under `frontend/`):
  - `frontend/src/lib/console/applied-line.ts` (`findAppliedLine`, the rule the three panels share: the line a build used on a day, worked out from the fitted rows, the switch, the lookback and the committed floor; found during execution: it gains `findBuiltLine`, the line the day's run record names where it holds one and otherwise the rule's line, and its header puts the record first)
  - `frontend/src/routes/console/judgement/+page.svelte` (hands the verdict split and the holdout margin `findAppliedLine(data.windowDay, ...)`, the line for the newest published day; found during execution: it hands all three panels `data.builtWith` and holds no line logic)
  - `frontend/src/routes/console/judgement/MergeLinePlot.svelte` (`ruleLine`, `findAppliedLine(viewport.end, ...)`: where the dashed rule goes when no fitted day is in the window; found during execution: it takes that line as a prop, `builtWith`, so `configuredLine` goes and the panel reads only the band from `knobs`)
  - `frontend/src/routes/console/judgement/+page.server.ts` (the route's loader: it reads each published day's day file through `loadDay` and the fitted rows through `fittedLines`, and no run record; found during execution: it reads the newest published day's recorded line through `readRecordedLine` and works out `builtWith` once, with `findBuiltLine`; `configuredLine` goes)
  - `frontend/src/lib/server/payload.ts` (`loadManifests` reads one `run.json` a published day inside a window while the site is built, for the console home and Hardware; its `RunRecord` keeps no `same_story_floor_applied`; changed only if Fowler rules that the read goes through it; unchanged, found during execution: Fowler ruled that the read has a file of its own, decision 1)
  - `frontend/tests/applied-line.spec.ts` (row L38's Oracle, which drives `findAppliedLine` on rows it writes; found during execution: `findBuiltLine`'s cases join it, and so does row L30's case of a held day inside the lookback)
  - `frontend/tests/console-window.spec.ts` (row L30's merge-line cases, which hand `MergeLinePlot` the rows, the switch and the lookback; held by L35 and L40 while they run; found during execution: row L30's four render cases leave, three of them already in `applied-line.spec.ts` with the same inputs and answers and the fourth moved there; one render case checks that the panel draws the line it is handed; the merge-line props carry `builtWith`)
  - `frontend/tests/console-run-health.spec.ts` (holds `loadManifests` to a manifest it writes; changed only if `loadManifests` changes; unchanged, found during execution)
  - `docs/concepts/console-design/the-rules-every-console-chart-obeys.md` (its holdout section says the line the newest published day was built with is the rule's answer: `floor_min` with the switch off, and otherwise the newest line a fit applied in the days a build looks back over; found during execution: the line is the one the day's last build wrote down, and the rule answers only where the record holds none)
  - `docs/architecture/publishing/autotune-content-similarity.md` (says the run writes down which line grouped it, and why; found during execution: it says Judgement reads it, and why the record comes first)

  - `frontend/tests/recorded-line.spec.ts` (new, found during execution: the reader's logic cases, on run records the test writes)

CI: the pull request runs the console specs; every group runs on the merge push.
- **Oracle:** on runs and rows the test builds (Table D, D3): a day whose build recorded one line while the fitted rows' rule names another. The verdict split, the holdout margin, and the merge line's dashed rule when no fitted day is in the window each name the recorded line. On `origin/main` they name the rule's line, which is what lets this check fail. It cannot settle that a build recorded the line it grouped at: `backend/idhazh/stages/assemble.py` writes the record, and no console test reaches it.
- **Follow-ups** (found during execution):
  - Reader: the note on "What the judge said about the line" says "the line the day was built with", while its pairs come from several days and the other two panels say "the newest day". Once days have different lines, a reader takes "the day" as each pair's own day, and the split does not work that way. Reader's words: "Every judged pair, split by whether its score cleared the line the newest day was built with and by what the judge said about it." A Level 1 row.
  - Reader, checked against the code: the merge line's solid line is each fitted row's `applied` line, and the panel's note calls it "the score two stories had to reach that day to be read as one story". The nightly fit writes a line whether the switch is on or off (`backend/idhazh/stages/set_merge_line.py` reads no switch, and `.github/workflows/llm-council.yml` runs every night), so the first night a fit moves the line while the switch is off, the solid line and its note name a line no build used. It is this row's fault on a different mark, and it can show with nobody turning the switch on; each day's run record holds the line its builds used. A row of its own, with Fowler and Reader.
  - Fowler: a test that renders the Judgement page itself on data it builds, so one check reads the line all three panels draw (Rejected alternatives, 7). Today the rule and the reader are held by logic cases, the merge line by a render case, and review reads the page's three bindings to `data.builtWith`. A Level 1 row once L42 merges, because the page's window control is in files L42 holds.
  - `stage_assemble` in `backend/idhazh/stages/assemble.py` records the line it chose even where it leaves a day that predates fragments as it is (`predates_fragments`), so running such a day again with the switch on would record a line that did not group it. Only days published before runs filed their own blocks can do this, and none of them is the newest published day. Fowler places it.
  - `docs/how-to/run-the-gates.md` and `docs/concepts/glossary.md` say three contract tests hold the frontend's hand copies in step; `docs/architecture/contracts/schemas.md` lists five now, four of them before this row. A Level 0 row.

**Decisions**

| # | Decision | Authority |
| --- | --- | --- |
| 1 | Fowler rules the read: which file the console reads the recorded line from, whether that crosses from code into published data (CLAUDE.md section 4), how a day with more than one build names its line, and the read's fixed-size input (Guardrail #12). What the search found: the console already reads each published day's `run.json` while the site is built, through `loadManifests`, for the console home and Hardware, and the Judgement route reads none; the site stages no `run.json`; a day's `run.json` holds one record for each build, and once the switch is on, two builds of one day can record different lines when one runs after the council filed (reasoned from the rule; every committed record holds 0.94). Ruled: the route reads the newest published day's `run.json` by name, through `readRecordedLine` in a file of its own, `frontend/src/lib/server/recorded-line.ts`, never through `loadManifests`, whose other readers would carry a field they never use and whose replacement, `PublicRunDay`, holds no line. It reads a committed file under `frontend/public/` while the site is built, which section 4 allows: nothing is staged or fetched, and one number goes into the prerendered page; a contract test holds the two keys it reads to `RunRecord`, because a renamed key would otherwise send the panels back to the rule with no error. Each build groups the whole day again, so the record with the newest `completed_at` names the day's line; a tie goes to the later record in the list, and a record with no time counts as the oldest. Where that record holds no line, or the file is missing or cannot be read, the panels name the rule's line with today's words, and an unreadable file warns in the build log, naming the date. The input is one file, which gains one record for each build of that day and nothing as the archive grows. One pure function, `findBuiltLine` in `applied-line.ts`, gives the recorded line or else the rule's; the loader calls it once, for the newest published day, and the page hands that one number, `builtWith`, to all three panels. Table C, C1 does not fire: no contract and no frontend copy of one moves, and `payload.ts`'s `RunRecord` is a reader's view that nothing persists | Fowler, 2026-10-08 |
| 2 | Reader chooses any words the ruling needs (CLAUDE.md section 14). Ruled: no word changes. "Built with" now names the line the day's last build wrote down, which is the version of the day a reader sees, so every label and sentence that names the line stays true; the words for a day whose record holds no line stay as they are, because no real build reaches that state and the build log reports a record it cannot read | Reader, 2026-10-08, in one ruling that agrees with Fowler's, so no debate round ran |
| 3 | The fault is row L38's report, finding 1, recorded in L38's section | Row L38's report; plan owner, 2026-10-08 |
| 4 | It lands before the merge switch, `assemble.same_story.adaptive_dedup_threshold.enabled` in `config/idhazh.json`, is turned on. Its committed value, `false`, keeps the fault from showing: every build groups at the committed floor, and the rule names that floor too | Plan owner, 2026-10-08 |
| 5 | L52 waits for L38 (#1439), which moved the rule into `applied-line.ts`, and for L35 and L40, which hold `frontend/tests/console-window.spec.ts` while they run | Plan owner, 2026-10-08 |
| 6 | Level 3: three panels on Judgement start to depend on `same_story_floor_applied`, a field the pipeline writes into each published day's run record and no console route reads today, so a change to what the pipeline records moves those panels (CLAUDE.md section 6: code and published data). No persisted shape moves, because the field is on the record already; if the ruling needs `RunManifest` or a frontend copy of it to change, Table C, C1 stops the row | Plan owner, 2026-10-08 |
| 7 | Row L30's rejected alternative 3, working the line out on the route and handing the panel one number, is taken now. Its reason no longer holds: every window ends on the newest published day (Table D, D5), so the window's last day is not the panel's to choose, and with `console.today_anchor` at `centre` it can be a day no build ran, so the merge line names the line the newest published day was built with, as the other two panels do. The rule stays driven by logic cases, which is the other half of that reason | Fowler, 2026-10-08 |
| 8 | The Oracle, on runs and rows the tests build (Table D, D3). `recorded-line.spec.ts` (new, `logic`) writes a day's `run.json` and reads it: a build that finished at 18:00 and recorded 0.937 and one that finished at 23:10 and recorded 0.940 give 0.940 in either list order; a tie gives the later record and a record with no time counts as the oldest (0.937); builds from before the record carried a line give no line; and a missing file gives no line in silence, while a file that is not JSON, lists no run, or records a line that is not a number gives no line and warns, naming the day. `applied-line.spec.ts` gains `findBuiltLine`: a recorded 0.940 beats the rule's 0.937, a recorded 0.937 beats the rule's 0.940 with the switch now off, and with no record each of the rule's six cases gives its own line. In `console-window.spec.ts` the merge line, handed 0.940 while its rows, switch and lookback say 0.937, draws its rule at 0.940 with Reader's words unchanged. On `origin/main` at 173f3f32a, in a copy outside the checkout: both logic specs stop at import, the merge-line case draws the rule at 0.937, and the page's own choice for the same inputs, `findAppliedLine` for the newest published day, gives the verdict split and the holdout margin 0.937 where the build recorded 0.940. On this branch all pass: 14 in `applied-line.spec.ts`, 9 in `recorded-line.spec.ts`, and 100 in `console-window.spec.ts`. No test renders the page itself (Follow-ups); a smoke on a canary build with 0.937 planted on its last build's record, with the switch off, showed all three panels at 0.937 at the 1- and 7-day presets | Found during execution (Table D, D3) |
| 9 | Two commits: first the merge line takes its line as a prop and the page hands it the same answer as before, so nothing a reader sees moves; then the record comes first, with the reader, `findBuiltLine`, `builtWith`, the three bindings, the contract test and the docs | Fowler, 2026-10-08 |

| 2 | Keep the rule, and change when the council files or when Pages rebuilds | Neither is the console's to set: the council's clock is in `.github/workflows/llm-council.yml`, and Pages builds `main`'s tip by the rule in `.github/workflows/pages.yml`. Either move leaves the rule working out what the record already holds | A change to a workflow this plan does not own, and the rule still a second account of what a build did | Plan owner, 2026-10-08 |
| 3 | Read the line through `loadManifests`, adding it to `payload.ts`'s `RunRecord` | The console home, Hardware and `machine-counters.ts` would carry a field they never use, two specs that test other things would change, and Judgement would depend on the reader that the `run-days` files are to replace, whose `PublicRunDay` holds no line | One field on `RunRecord` and no new file | Fowler, 2026-10-08 |
| 4 | The last record in the list names the day's line | A build that is run again replaces its record where it stands, so an older build can stay last in the list | One key fewer to read; it gives the same answer on every committed day read (6 to 8 Oct 2026) | Fowler, 2026-10-08 |

| 7 | Render `+page.svelte` itself in a test, so one check reads the three panels' lines on one page | It needs built data for every panel, a stand-in for `$app/paths`, and `WindowControlSource.svelte` and `window-slot.ts`, which row L42 holds now | A check at the page's own level, which no test has today: a row of its own once L42 merges (Follow-ups) | Fowler, 2026-10-08 |

### Row #L53 - Hardware reads a machine record with no job the same way everywhere

- **Scope:** On Hardware, every reader of the machine record takes a row whose job cell is empty the same way, once a measurement on the committed data shows that packed rows lack that cell. The row measures first, and changes nothing if no packed row lacks it. Level 1.
- **The fault** (row L47's report, 2026-10-08; Fowler's reasoning, not measured; read again on `origin/main` at 62da7be9a): `fingerprintsOf` in `frontend/src/lib/server/host-fingerprint.ts` skips a machine record row whose job cell is empty, because `serverJob` finds no job in it, while `machineCounters` in `frontend/src/lib/server/machine-counters.ts` reads an empty job as the work job, `READ_JOB`. `fleetJobs` in `frontend/src/lib/charts/fleet.ts` and the published series' `PUBLISHED_JOB` in `backend/idhazh/telemetry/publish/machine.py` read it as `machineCounters` does. Rows written today carry `work`, the default of `job` on `HostFingerprintRow` (`backend/idhazh/contracts/host_fingerprint.py`), so it matters only if older packed rows lack that cell. Such a row would count toward a run but not toward the machine record's days, `machineDays` in `frontend/src/routes/console/machine/+page.server.ts`. Reasoned from the same code: a day whose rows all lacked it, and that published articles, would also read as a day whose measurement was lost, because `lostDays` takes the days `machineRecordDays` names that `fingerprintsOf` does not. No frontend test builds a row whose job cell is empty.
- **Files touched** (found by a search on `origin/main` at 62da7be9a for `fingerprintsOf`, `serverJob`, `READ_JOB`, `PUBLISHED_JOB`, `row.job` and `fleetJobs` under `frontend/`, `backend/idhazh/` and `docs/`; search again at dispatch):
  - `frontend/src/lib/server/host-fingerprint.ts` (`fingerprintsOf`, through `serverJob`, skips the row; changed only if decision 1 finds such rows and Fowler rules that it reads them)
  - `frontend/src/lib/server/machine-counters.ts` (`machineCounters` reads an empty job as `READ_JOB`; changed only if Fowler rules that every reader skips such a row)
  - `frontend/src/lib/charts/fleet.ts` (`fleetJobs` reads an empty job as `work`; changed only if Fowler rules the skip)
  - `frontend/src/routes/console/machine/+page.server.ts` (takes `machineDays` and `recordedDays` from `fingerprintsOf`, and `counters` from `machineCounters`; changed only if the ruling puts the rule in one place that every reader calls)
  - `frontend/tests/console-machine-cards.spec.ts` (the Oracle, beside "a day whose machine rows name no machine is a day the record RAN", which builds rows for `fingerprintsOf` and `machineRecordDays`)
  - Read, no change: `backend/idhazh/telemetry/publish/machine.py`, whose `fold_shards` reads an empty job as `PUBLISHED_JOB`; `backend/idhazh/contracts/host_fingerprint.py`, whose `job` defaults to `work`. No page under `docs/` says how a reader takes an empty job cell
- **Acceptance gates:** local: the measurement (decision 1), with its question and its answer in the row's report. If the row changes code: `npm --prefix frontend run test:changed -- --spec console-machine-cards.spec.ts`, then the specs it selects for the changed files; `npm --prefix frontend run check`; the browser smoke of Hardware at each window preset on the canary build. CI: the pull request runs the console specs; every group runs on the merge push.
- **Oracle:** the measurement comes first, outside any test: the count of machine record rows whose job cell is empty, over every packed day. In the console's Data explorer, `SELECT count(*) FILTER (WHERE coalesce(job, '') = '') AS no_job, count(*) AS rows_read FROM "host-fingerprint"`, over a window that starts on or before the record's first day, asks it. If the count is 0, it is the row's record, and nothing changes. If it is more, the Oracle is in `console-machine-cards.spec.ts`, on rows the test builds (Table D, D3): a run whose machine rows leave the job cell empty is read the same way by every reader Fowler names, so its day counts toward a run and toward the machine record's days, or toward neither. On `origin/main` `fingerprintsOf` leaves the row out while `machineCounters` keeps it, which is what lets this check fail. It cannot settle which reading is right, which Fowler rules (decision 2).

**Decisions**

| # | Decision | Authority |
| --- | --- | --- |
| 1 | Measure first, on the committed data through the console's Data explorer or an operator utility, never in a test (CLAUDE.md section 13): how many packed machine record rows have an empty job cell. If none, the row closes with the measurement and changes nothing | Measured 2026-10-08, by reading the committed packed files directly with DuckDB rather than through the console (`origin/main` at `bd3795b33e42e7dc14a8cbcbf95f9b67ac344533`, which holds the same `state/compact/host-fingerprint/` files as the commit the search read, `62da7be9a`): `SELECT count(*) FILTER (WHERE coalesce(job, '') = '') AS no_job, count(*) AS rows_read FROM "host-fingerprint"` over `state/compact/host-fingerprint/daily/*/*/*.parquet`, a window that starts on 1 Sep 2026, before the record's first row on 17 Sep 2026, through its newest packed day, 6 Oct 2026. It read 528 rows; 0 had an empty job cell. No packed row lacks the cell, so the row closes with this measurement; plan owner, 2026-10-08 |
| 2 | If some, Fowler rules one reading for every reader of the cell, `fingerprintsOf`, `machineCounters` and `fleetJobs`, and where that rule lives; a test on rows it builds pins it (CLAUDE.md section 14) | To be ruled at dispatch (Fowler), if decision 1 finds such rows |
| 3 | The fault is Fowler's, reasoned during row L47 and not measured. The search found `fleetJobs` and the published series reading the cell as `machineCounters` does, so `fingerprintsOf` is the one reader that skips it | Fowler, 2026-10-08 (row L47's report); plan author, 2026-10-08 (the search) |
| 4 | Level 1: how one route reads one cell of one record; a wrong version is obvious and local, and with no such row nothing changes | Plan owner, 2026-10-08 |

**Rejected alternatives**

| # | Option | Why rejected | What it would cost to take | Authority |
| --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |
| 1 | As today | If older packed rows lack the cell, Hardware counts such a row toward a run and leaves its day out of the machine record's days | Nothing to build, and possibly a machine record one day short, or a day read as lost, beside a run the counters count | Fowler, 2026-10-08 (row L47's report) |
| 2 | Change a reader without measuring | The fault is reasoned, not measured, and rows written today carry `work`, so a change could move a reader for a case no committed row holds | One reader changed blind | Plan owner, 2026-10-08 |
| 3 | Measure in a test over the committed data | A test checks what the code does, never what the data holds (Table D, D3; CLAUDE.md section 13), and a read of the committed archive grows with every run (Guardrail #12) | A test that a pipeline run alone can turn red, and that reads more with every run | The owner, 2026-10-05 (Table D, D3) |

### Row #L54 - Failure rate against volume says it is waiting, or that its data did not come, before it says nothing was planned

- **Scope:** On Pipelines, "Failure rate against volume" says that it is waiting while its month file has not been read, and that its data did not come when the file did not arrive, as Jony, Susan and Reader rule. It says that no item was planned only of a window it has read. Level 1.
- **The fault** (row L35's Follow-up, Susan's, 2026-10-08; read again on `origin/main` at d8961d8b2, after #1447): the panel, `frontend/src/lib/components/FailurePanels.svelte`, prints "No item was planned in {span}, so there is no rate to give and no volume to give it against." whenever the window holds no row (`load.empty`). The page holds no telemetry row until a month file lands, so the panel prints it before a script has read the window, and again when the month file did not arrive: a page still loading, or missing its data, states a fact about the pipeline. `docs/architecture/publishing/console-charts.md`, in "An empty chart box says which nothing it holds", asks a chart whose data is pending to say it is loading, and one whose data did not load to explain the failure. `frontend/src/lib/components/Viewport.svelte` draws this panel and the two below it with no reserved box, on purpose: its comment says each says what is empty about it, and that a month that did not arrive is said once, above the panels. The two panels above it, "What is failing, by stage" and "Where an item's time went", stand in a reserved box (`Reserved`) that says when a month did not come back, and since row L35 (#1447) it waits until the page has read the window.
- **Files touched** (found by a search on `origin/main` at 62da7be9a, and again at d8961d8b2 after #1447, for "No item was planned", `load.empty`, `data-failure-empty`, `FailurePanels`, `Reserved` and `telemetryState` under `frontend/` and `docs/`; search again at dispatch):
  - `frontend/src/lib/components/FailurePanels.svelte` (the sentence, printed whenever `load.empty`, whatever the fetch did)
  - `frontend/src/lib/components/Viewport.svelte` (draws the panel with no reserved box and hands it the rows; found during execution: passes the page's fetch state to the failure panel without changing the other panels)
  - `frontend/src/routes/console/+page.svelte` (holds `ready` and the fetch's state, `telemetryState`, which the two panels above it hand their `Reserved` boxes, and hands `Viewport` the rows; found during execution: passes its fetch state to the failure panel)
  - `frontend/tests/console-failure.spec.ts` (the Oracle, beside "a window holding nothing renders, and says so rather than drawing zero", which serves rows the test builds through `openServed`; found during execution: adds held-file and 404 cases)
  - `frontend/tests/support/served-telemetry.ts` (`openServed` answers every month file the page asks for once the test has built its rows; changed only if the Oracle needs a month it holds back or refuses)
  - `docs/architecture/publishing/console-charts.md` (its "Failure rate against volume" paragraph, and "An empty chart box says which nothing it holds")
  - Read, no change unless the ruling reaches them: `frontend/src/lib/components/Reserved.svelte`, the box the two panels above it use; `frontend/src/lib/console/waiting.ts`, which writes the sentence for each state of the fetch; `frontend/src/lib/components/BandDistance.svelte` and `frontend/src/lib/components/FailureList.svelte`, which `Viewport.svelte` leaves outside a box for the same reason; `frontend/tests/console.spec.ts`, whose "panning to a month with no rows leaves a visible gap" pins the sentence for a window that was read and holds no row; `docs/concepts/console-design/the-mark-shapes-a-panel-may-reach-for.md`, whose section "A console panel reserves its room, and names which nothing it is holding" says a reserved box and its failure state are one decision
- **Acceptance gates:** local: `npm --prefix frontend run test:changed -- --spec console-failure.spec.ts`, then the specs it selects for the changed files; `npm --prefix frontend run check`; `doc_load.py` on each changed page; the browser smoke of Pipelines at the 1- and 14-day presets, with its month files held back, answered 404 and answered with rows. CI: the pull request runs the console specs; every group runs on the merge push.
- **Oracle:** in `console-failure.spec.ts`, with the monthly request held and with it answered 404, the panel prints Reader's exact sentences, "Reading the monthly files. This chart is not ready yet." and "This chart is unavailable.", and prints neither a quiet sentence nor a chart. For a window the test reads successfully and builds with no rows, it prints the whole sentence, "No item was planned in these 7 days, so there is no rate to give and no volume to give it against." (Table D, D3). On `origin/main` the held and 404 cases print the quiet-window sentence, which is what lets these checks fail.

**Decisions**

| # | Decision | Authority |
| --- | --- | --- |
| 1 | The failure-rate panel stays in the item-telemetry viewport without a reserved box. Its short waiting and unavailable messages fit in its own place, while a box would add a fixed slot to a group whose panels flow to their own empty states | Jony and Susan, 2026-10-09 |
| 2 | While the files are being read, the panel says "Reading the monthly files. This chart is not ready yet." When a required file does not arrive, it says "This chart is unavailable." The shared line above the panels names the failed month. A successfully read empty window keeps its existing quiet sentence | Reader, 2026-10-09 |
| 3 | The fault is row L35's Follow-up, which Susan found | Row L35's report; plan owner, 2026-10-08 |
| 4 | L54 waits for L35 (#1447), whose reserved box is the precedent and which changed `frontend/src/routes/console/+page.svelte` | Plan owner, 2026-10-08 |
| 5 | Level 1: what one panel says while its data is loading or did not arrive; a wrong version is obvious and local | Plan owner, 2026-10-08 |
| 6 | The route passes its telemetry state through the viewport. The failure panel shows a state sentence only when it has no rows, so available rows remain visible while other files are being read | Found during execution |

**Rejected alternatives**

| # | Option | Why rejected | What it would cost to take | Authority |
| --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |
| 1 | As today | A page still loading, or missing its month file, says that no item was planned in the window | Nothing to build, and a panel that states a fact about a pipeline it has not read | Row L35's report; plan owner, 2026-10-08 |

### Row #L55 - The agreement chart shows a share above its top and breaks at a day with no pair

- **Scope:** On Judgement's agreement chart, a share above the axis top is not drawn as the top's value, and no line joins across a day that read no pair, as Jony rules. Level 1.
- **The faults** (row L40's Follow-ups 1 and 2, 2026-10-08; read again on `origin/main` at 62da7be9a):
  1. A share above the axis top is drawn on the top edge. `heightOf` in `frontend/src/routes/console/judgement/JudgeAgreement.svelte` places a share at `Math.min(share, corridor[1])`, and the axis top is the larger of the two marks (`agreementCorridor` in `frontend/src/lib/console/merge-line.ts`). In row L40's planted smoke, 19 Sep's 40% sat on the 35% edge and read as 35%, while the strip said 40%. The chart shows a value it does not hold.
  2. A day that read no pair gets no column: `read` keeps only the days with a pair, so `runsOf` joins the dots on either side of such a day. Row L40's rule forbids a line across a day under the floor, and `docs/architecture/publishing/console-charts.md` asks a chart to draw the selected window, its empty days included.
- **Files touched** (found by a search on `origin/main` at 62da7be9a for `heightOf`, `corridor`, `read`, `columnsX`, `runsOf` and `agreementCorridor`, and under `docs/` for the chart's rules; searched again at dispatch, on `origin/main` at 2c1dce9d4 after L51 merged, which found no other caller of `agreementCorridor` or of the chart's own `read`, `marks` and `runsOf`):
  - `frontend/src/routes/console/judgement/JudgeAgreement.svelte` (`heightOf`, `read`, `columnsX` and `runsOf`; held by L34 and L51 while each runs)
  - `frontend/tests/console-window.spec.ts` (the Oracle, beside row L40's `DOT_CASES`, which name each column's dots by the axis label they sit on and each line by the days it joins; held by L34, L42, L45, L51 and L52 while each runs; found during execution: L42 (#1464) added a case after this row's dispatch, in a part of the file this row does not touch, and merged cleanly)
  - `docs/architecture/publishing/console-charts.md` (its rule that a chart draws the selected window with its empty days, and its section "A share under the floor gets no mark"; it records Jony's ruling)
  - `frontend/src/lib/console/merge-line.ts` (`agreementCorridor`; Jony's ruling moves the axis, so this is no longer read-only; held by L45 and L51 while each runs; found during execution: no change to `JudgeDay`, `AgreementLimits` or any other export)
  - Found during execution, because Reader's two words need a column-specific reason a shared helper had only one word for: `frontend/src/lib/charts/readout.ts` and `frontend/src/lib/components/ChartReadout.svelte` (`notMeasuredAt`, an optional per-column override of `notMeasured`; every existing caller omits it and is unaffected) and their own tests, `frontend/tests/console-readout-data.spec.ts` and `frontend/tests/merge-line.spec.ts` (`agreementCorridor`'s own unit test) and `frontend/tests/console-judgement-agreement.spec.ts` (its two axis assertions, pinned to the old fixed top)
- **Follow-ups** (found during execution, once every window day became a column):
  - Susan's sufficiency pass found that the 7-day preset now regularly draws a day with a real dot but no axis-tick label of its own, a pre-existing `frame.ts` sampling artifact (`chart.tick_density`, shared and untouched by this row) that this row's own change made frequent rather than rare; Jony's minimum correction prints that day's own date beside its topmost dot, raised clear of a dashed mark's own label where the two would otherwise compete for the plot's right edge (decisions 6 through 8). Fixed in this row; no further work.
  - Left open by Jony's own ruling on the fix above (decision 8), in the same file this row already owns: a stranded label can still land on a dashed mark's own label where BOTH of two things are true of one day - its label would otherwise sit close enough to the plot's right edge to reach a mark's label box (the case decision 8 fixes), AND that mark's own line sits close enough to the plot's own top margin that pushing the stranded label clear of the mark's box would require a position higher than the label's own top clamp allows, so the clamp pulls it back down into the box it was just pushed out of. In today's code the threshold is the mark's pixel position landing within `AXIS_LABEL_PX` (the shared axis-label size) of twice that amount from `box.top` (the plot's own top margin, both from `frame.ts`, read only); a mark that close to the top is reachable whenever the niced axis tops out only slightly above that mark's own value. No fix prescribed: Jony named the corner and left the resolution open rather than guess at one. Same file only (`frontend/src/routes/console/judgement/JudgeAgreement.svelte`, inside `strandedLabels`), no shared file touched - a Level 1 row, by this row's own reasoning (one chart, one local fix, obvious once seen); the owner places it and checks it against whichever row currently holds this file before dispatch.
- **Acceptance gates:** local: `npm --prefix frontend run test:changed -- --spec console-window.spec.ts`; `npm --prefix frontend run check`; `doc_load.py` on the page; the browser smoke of the Judgement route at the 1- and 7-day presets, on a build with fitted rows planted for the smoke, as row L40's smoke did, with a share above the top and a day that read no pair. CI: the pull request runs the console specs; every group runs on the merge push.
- **Oracle:** in `console-window.spec.ts`, beside row L40's `DOT_CASES`, on days the test builds (Table D, D3): a window that holds a day whose disagreed share is above the axis top draws that share as Jony rules, never as a plain dot at the top's label; and a window with a day that read no pair between two measured days draws that day as Jony rules, and neither line joins across it. Two more cases join them, added once Susan's and Jony's rulings below settled what a dropped tick's own day draws: a day with a dot but no axis tick, beside a day with no reading, carries its own date beside its topmost dot; and at a built 14-day worst case, a stranded label at a mark's own height, near the plot's right edge, is read clear of that mark's own label through each element's own rendered bounding box, never a fixed pixel guess. On `origin/main` the first two sit at the top's label and join lines across the day; the third and fourth find no stranded label at all, because it does not exist before this row - which is what lets each fail there too. It cannot settle what either day draws instead, which Jony rules (decision 1), nor the stranded label's own rule or its collision correction, which Jony also rules (decisions 7 and 8).

**Decisions**

| # | Decision | Authority |
| --- | --- | --- |
| 1 | Jony's ruling: the axis is niced from both dashed marks and from every share that draws a dot (`agreementCorridor` in `merge-line.ts`, through `linearAxis`), rounded outward to a whole tick step, so a share past a mark widens the axis instead of drawing pinned to the mark's own line; a healthy window (no share past either mark) niced to 0-40% instead of the old fixed 0-35%, which was measured to give the looser mark 24px of headroom on a 190px plot. Every day of the selected window is a column, read or not: a day with no reading draws no dot and carries no accessible-name group - it has nothing to name - and each rate's line breaks there, never joining across it, the same rule row L40 built for a day under the floor. The day still answers a pointer, a tap or an arrow key. A window where no day read any pair stays exactly as today: no columns, the "none" sentence, because a strip of columns that would all repeat one empty sentence has nothing to add. The strip rests on the newest day WITH a reading (the existing `'newest'` rule, already used elsewhere on the console), never on the window's last day: the newest one or two days of a real window are themselves often not yet packed. Where the chart left a dot out for a different reason (under the floor), that caption's own rule is unchanged and does not also count a day with no reading, which is not under the floor and whose own strip entry already says why it is empty (CLAUDE.md section 14) | Jony, 2026-10-09 |
| 2 | Reader's words: at rest, the heading note is always `, the newest day with numbers` (not conditionally the old `, the newest day`), because one resting rule should carry one note and this one stays true whether or not a newer, still-unread day sits to its right. A column with no reading prints one of two reasons in place of its two entries: `No readings came in for this day` where its own row read no pair (the page knows the count was zero), and `The numbers for this day are not here yet, or there are none` where the page holds no row for that day at all (not packed yet, or none ran - the page cannot tell which) (CLAUDE.md section 14) | Reader, 2026-10-09 |
| 3 | The faults are row L40's Follow-ups 1 and 2, each a Level 1 row in Jony's words. They are one row because both change how one chart places its days, in one file | Row L40's report; plan owner, 2026-10-08 |
| 4 | L55 waits for L51, which changes `JudgeAgreement.svelte` | Plan owner, 2026-10-08 |
| 5 | Level 1: what one chart draws for two kinds of day; a wrong version is obvious and local | Plan owner, 2026-10-08 |
| 6 | Susan's sufficiency ruling: the row's two core fixes ship clean in both themes and both widths - the gap reads as "no reading", not a bug, and the widened axis stays legible. One finding was required before merge, not deferred: at the 7-day preset, every day is now a column (decision 1), so `dayTicks`'s shared sampling ceiling (`chart.tick_density`, untouched by this row) regularly drops one day's own axis-tick label even though that day carries a real dot. The pointer and keyboard readout already name that day, but a reader who is only looking, with a mouse, on a screen, never reaches either - so the reading loss is the blocker, not the shared thinning itself, which stays exactly as it was | Susan, 2026-10-09 |
| 7 | Jony's correction for decision 6: a day's own date prints beside its topmost dot, `aria-hidden` since it repeats what the day's own accessible name already says, where that day's axis tick was dropped by the shared sampling and the day has an unread neighbour immediately before or after it in the window - reconciled from Jony's first wording ("both neighbours unread") to "at least one", to match the real smoke day this was built against, where only one side was unread. Jony also required one pre-existing, unrelated bug in the same tick-label loop fixed alongside it: the loop printed a tick's date even where the width-fitting pass had already blanked its text; gated on `tick.text`, matching the pattern `MergeLinePlot.svelte` (L52's file, not touched here) already uses | Jony, 2026-10-09 |
| 8 | A further collision Jony asked to confirm before shipping, found in a built 14-day worst case: a stranded label can land at a dashed mark's own height, where near the plot's right edge both labels compete for the same corner and sit on top of each other. Jony rejected two narrower fixes (rejected alternatives 6 and 7) and ruled: read each mark's own label as the small box it occupies, computed from `labelWidth` and `AXIS_LABEL_PX` (both already exported by the shared `frame.ts`, read only, not changed), and where a stranded label's own box would overlap one, raise it clear of that box before the existing top clamp runs. Left open, by the same ruling: a mark within about twice `AXIS_LABEL_PX` of the plot's own top could still collide with the top clamp itself - narrow enough to leave until it is hit, and not claimed solved here | Jony, 2026-10-09 |

**Rejected alternatives**

| # | Option | Why rejected | What it would cost to take | Authority |
| --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |
| 1 | As today | The chart draws a share above the top as the top's value, and a line through a day nobody measured | Nothing to build, and a dot that the strip above it contradicts | Row L40's report; plan owner, 2026-10-08 |
| 2 | A fixed axis that clips a share past the mark to the mark's own line, or draws no mark at all for it | The one reading the panel exists to show - how far past a mark a day went - is exactly the one a clipped or missing mark hides | A chart that looks calm on its worst day | Jony, 2026-10-09 |
| 3 | A day with no reading keeps no column, as today, with columns spaced only over the days that have one | The line still joins two read days across a day nobody measured, which the day-under-the-floor rule no longer permits for the day beside it | A chart whose x axis is not the window the operator chose | Jony, 2026-10-09 |
| 4 | Give an unread day's own `<g>` an accessible name too, the way a read day's dots carry one | There is nothing to name: no reading, no dot, no mark. A name invented for an empty column reads as a fact the page does not hold | An accessible-name group that announces nothing happened, on every unread day of every window | Jony, 2026-10-09 |
| 5 | Defer Susan's finding (decision 6) to a follow-up row, keeping L55 at its original two fixes, as this row's own escalation first recommended | Once a minimum correction looked cheap, and did not touch the shared `frame.ts` the finding was rooted in, the owner ruled a narrow Jony clarification round instead of a deferral | A chart that ships with a reading loss Susan called a blocker, on the strength of a deferral never put to Jony | Plan owner, 2026-10-09 |
| 6 | Hide the stranded label near the plot's right edge whenever it would be close enough to a mark's own label to collide, rather than computing where the two actually overlap | A reader loses the one date the stranded label exists to give back, silently, exactly at the window's widest, densest presets where it is needed most | A label that goes missing again, for a different reason than the one Susan found | Jony, 2026-10-09 |
| 7 | Move the stranded label sideways, off its dot, to dodge the mark label's own corner, instead of raising it | The label no longer sits over the dot it names, so a reader has to guess which dot a floating date belongs to | A date label that is unambiguous only until a chart has two dots close enough together to need the same dodge | Jony, 2026-10-09 |

### Row #L56 - One rule breaks a line at a gap on every console chart

- **Scope:** The five console charts that break a line at a gap with a loop of their own break it through one shared function, if Fowler rules one, and each chart keeps the breaks it draws today. Level 2.
- **The fault** (row L40's Follow-up 4, 2026-10-08; each loop found again by a search on `origin/main` at 62da7be9a): one rule, that a line breaks at a gap and never joins across it, is written five times, so a fix to one chart's break reaches none of the other four. Four of the loops keep only runs of two or more points; the stage timings chart's keeps a run of one, which it does not draw as a line, and it alone also breaks at a measured zero. `dateSeries` in `frontend/src/lib/charts/d3/dateSeries.ts` breaks its lines through d3's `line().defined()`, with no loop of its own. The five:
  1. `segments` in `frontend/src/lib/components/FailurePanels.svelte`, the failure chart on Pipelines: a stage's rate line breaks where its rate is null.
  2. `yieldRuns` in `frontend/src/lib/charts/run-yield.ts`, whose runs `segments` in `frontend/src/lib/components/RunYield.svelte` places, in `frontend/src/routes/console/RunHealthPanel.svelte` on Pipelines: the share-published line breaks at a day with no share.
  3. `runs` in `frontend/src/lib/components/StageTimings.svelte`, the stage timings chart on Pipelines: a stage's line breaks at a day with no number or a measured zero.
  4. `proposedRuns` in `frontend/src/routes/console/judgement/MergeLinePlot.svelte`, the merge line on Judgement: the dotted series breaks at a held day.
  5. `runsOf` in `frontend/src/routes/console/judgement/JudgeAgreement.svelte`, the agreement chart on Judgement: each rate's line breaks where its dot is left out.
- **Files touched** (found by a search on `origin/main` at 62da7be9a under `frontend/src/` for the five names, for loops that start a new run where a value is missing, and for `.defined(`, and under `frontend/tests/` for each chart's break cases; search again at dispatch):
  - `frontend/src/lib/components/FailurePanels.svelte` (`segments`; held by L54 while it runs)
  - `frontend/src/lib/charts/run-yield.ts` (`yieldRuns`)
  - `frontend/src/lib/components/RunYield.svelte` (`segments`, which places the runs `yieldRuns` returns; changed only if `yieldRuns` goes)
  - `frontend/src/lib/components/StageTimings.svelte` (`runs`; held by L34 while it runs)
  - `frontend/src/routes/console/judgement/MergeLinePlot.svelte` (`proposedRuns`; held by L34, L45 and L52 while each runs)
  - `frontend/src/routes/console/judgement/JudgeAgreement.svelte` (`runsOf`; held by L51, L55 and L57 while each runs)
  - the shared function's own module and its logic case, if Fowler rules one: where they live is his ruling (decision 1), and a new spec joins the `logic` list in `frontend/scripts/test-groups.ts`
  - `docs/architecture/publishing/console-charts.md` (its section "A share under the floor gets no mark" says a share's line breaks at its column and never joins across it; changed only if the ruling names the shared function there)
  - Read, no change, each chart's existing break cases: `frontend/tests/console-failure.spec.ts` ("a day too thin to divide breaks the line rather than drawing a share", which checks that the rate is null, not the drawn line); `frontend/tests/run-yield.spec.ts` ("the line breaks at a day with no share rather than crossing it" and "a lone day is a dot and not a one-point line"); `frontend/tests/console.spec.ts` ("a stage with no number draws a gap, never a plunge to the axis floor"); `frontend/tests/console-judgement-line.spec.ts` ("a held day breaks the proposed series and keeps the applied one"); `frontend/tests/console-window.spec.ts` (row L40's `DOT_CASES`)
- **Acceptance gates:** local: `npm --prefix frontend run test:changed -- --list`, then the specs it selects for the changed files, at least the five specs above and the shared function's; `npm --prefix frontend run check`; the browser smoke of Pipelines and Judgement at the 1- and 7-day presets. CI: the pull request runs the console specs; every group runs on the merge push.
- **Oracle:** each chart's existing break cases, named under Files touched, pass unchanged, plus one logic case for the shared function, on points the test builds (Table D, D3), with every run written out. On `origin/main` the logic case stops at import, because the function is new, which is what lets this check fail; the chart cases pass on both sides, which shows that no break moved. It cannot settle the failure chart's or the merge line's call: no case reads the failure chart's drawn line, and the merge line's case reads the canary, which has never fitted a line, so it returns before it checks a break.

**Decisions**

| # | Decision | Authority |
| --- | --- | --- |

What the search found: four loops break at a missing value and keep runs of two or more; `StageTimings.svelte`'s also breaks at a measured zero, and keeps a run of one that it does not draw as a line; and `dateSeries` breaks its lines through d3's `line().defined()`, a rule the library already holds (Guardrail #8) | To be ruled at dispatch (Fowler) |

| 5 | Level 2, set from the search: five charts on two routes, Pipelines and Judgement, would draw through one function, so a wrong version breaks every one of their lines at once, and the row checks each chart by name. Row L40's Follow-up named Level 1 before the search counted the charts and their routes | Plan author, 2026-10-08 |

### Row #L57 - The agreement panel's numbers print the way the console's other numbers do

- **Scope:** On Judgement's agreement panel, a verdict never calls a share past its mark while it prints the mark's own figure, in Reader's words, and a count of 1,000 or more prints with a thousands separator, as the console's other counts do. Level 1.
- **The faults** (row L51's report, Follow-ups 1 and 3, 2026-10-08; each found again by a search on `origin/main` at 6cbc07926):
  1. A share just above its mark rounds to the mark's own figure. `disagreedVerdict` and `bothVerdict` in `frontend/src/routes/console/judgement/JudgeAgreement.svelte` judge each share against its mark before rounding, by the run's own test, and print the share through `wholePercent` (`frontend/src/lib/console/merge-line.ts`), which rounds it to a whole percent. The plot labels round each mark's figure the same way, through the panel's own `percent`. So 7 of 46 pairs, 15.2 percent, prints "The 15% that disagreed is past its mark." beside the label "15% - the "disagreed" mark". The sentence for days held because of the judge, the `past-mark` state, can do the same: it prints the window's disagreed share through `rateWithDenominator`, such as "15% of 46 pairs", and says that "a rate was past its mark". Reader asks that a verdict never disagree with the figure on screen.
  2. A count of 1,000 or more prints with no thousands separator. `rateWithDenominator` prints "{n} pairs" and `describeUnclear` prints "the {n} that agreed", each with the count as it is, so at the 30-day window the panel printed "20% of 1390 pairs" and "5% of the 1118 that agreed". The merge share on the same route, `mergeRate` in `merge-line.ts`, prints its count through `grouped` (`frontend/src/lib/charts/series.ts`), such as "1,000 stories", as the record panel (`RecordGates.svelte`) and the verdict split (`VerdictSplit.svelte`) do. At about 100 pairs a day, a 30-day window passes 1,000 pairs.
- **Files touched** (found by a search on `origin/main` at 6cbc07926 under `frontend/src/`, `frontend/tests/` and `docs/` for `wholePercent`, `rateWithDenominator`, `describeUnclear`, `disagreedVerdict`, `bothVerdict`, "past its mark", "that agreed", `grouped(`, `plural(` and `toLocaleString`; search again at dispatch):
  - `frontend/src/routes/console/judgement/JudgeAgreement.svelte` (`disagreedVerdict` and `bothVerdict`, which print a share beside "past its mark" or "inside its mark"; the `past-mark` sentence; the plot labels, whose figures `percent` rounds; held by L55 and L56 while each runs)
  - `frontend/src/lib/console/merge-line.ts` (`rateWithDenominator` and `describeUnclear`, which print their counts as they are; `wholePercent`, which rounds every share the panel and the merge share print; held by L45 while it runs)

  - Read, no change unless Reader's words reach it: `frontend/tests/console-judgement-agreement.spec.ts`, whose "every share prints its denominator in the same sentence" reads every whole percent on the panel and looks for its count beside it
  - Read, no change: `frontend/src/lib/charts/series.ts`, whose `grouped` separates thousands by hand, because `toLocaleString` reads the machine's locale and two builds have to agree; `frontend/src/lib/format.ts`, whose `plural` prints a count as it is, and which the panel calls only for a count under `console.min_attempts_for_rate`

  - At 7 days, 7 of 46 pairs disagreed, 39 agreed and none could not tell: `In these 7 days, just above 15% of 46 pairs disagreed with their own second reading, and 0% of the 39 that agreed could not tell. The share that disagreed is just above its 15% mark.`
  - At 7 days, the same reading with one day held because of the judge: `The two readings disagreed on just above 15% of 46 pairs in these 7 days. No line was fitted on 1 of 7 days, because a rate was past its mark on that day.`
  - At 1 day, 1,390 judged pairs, 1,118 agreed and 56 could not tell: `In this one day, 20% of 1,390 pairs disagreed with their own second reading, and 5% of the 1,118 that agreed could not tell. The 20% that disagreed is past its mark.`
  - A fourth rendered case checks the close-above wording against the "could not tell" mark and its agreed denominator. Logic cases hold the separator, both sides of a mark, exact equality, a difference smaller than one decimal, a changed mark and the counts-only floor. An archived actual base renders `The 15% that disagreed is past its mark.`, the held sentence's `15% of 46 pairs`, and the unseparated `1390 pairs` and `the 1118 that agreed`. Each required case fails there and passes on the branch.

Both-past keeps its existing verdict. The held-day sentence uses the same share words and still names that day's hold. Dashed mark labels do not change. A plan whose `through` is before the requested window reads no day, as `endDay` already recognizes, but its `startDay` still contributes the window's first day.

  - `frontend/tests/console-data-explorer.spec.ts` (found during execution: the generated-ledger page case checks the action line and rendered answer note for a selected ledger that stopped before the window)
  - `docs/architecture/publishing/how-the-query-door-answers-a-written-question.md` (found during execution: the shared reader's span rule excludes a ledger that read no day from both bounds)
- **Acceptance gates:** local: `npm --prefix frontend run test:changed -- --list`, then the selected ledger-lifecycle spec; `npm --prefix frontend run check`. Coordinate with plan 55's owner before changing anything under `frontend/src/lib/data/`. CI: the pull request runs the selected frontend specs; every group runs on the merge push.
- **Oracle:** Build one selected ledger whose `through` is before the window and a second whose first actual read is later than the window start but whose range overlaps the window. The answer's `readFrom` and the Data explorer's line name the second ledger's first read day, not the day supplied by the ledger that read nothing. Keep the existing cases where ledgers overlap and where one answer ends early; do not add disjoint-range counting.

### Row #L62 - The agreement chart's date and mark labels stay clear near the plot top

- **Scope:** Keep a measured day's local date label and a dashed mark's label readable and separate when both reach the plot's upper-right corner, using the smallest placement Jony rules for this chart. Level 1; no persisted shape changes.
- **The fault** (L55's discovered defect, explicitly left open in #1483's Follow-ups and decision 8; read at branch head `edbaeea98aa28df3012178968fe73dc7ad60780f`, not claimed finished): `strandedLabels` prints a measured day's date when the axis dropped its tick and an immediately adjacent day has no reading. Near the plot's right edge, the date can reach a dashed mark's label. L55 raises the date clear of that label's box, then clamps its position to `box.top + 2`. Where the mark itself is near the plot top, the clamp can pull the date back into the box it just cleared. A quiet window whose rounded axis ends only slightly above the higher mark can reach this case. Advisor willingness to leave it open is not evidence that the labels are separate.
- **Files touched** (named files read on the L55 branch; verify symbols and file lists again after its exact merge):
  - `frontend/src/routes/console/judgement/JudgeAgreement.svelte` (`strandedLabels`, `markLabelBoxes` and the rendered local date and dashed-mark labels; held by L55, L57 and L56 while each runs)
  - `frontend/tests/console-window.spec.ts` (the generated-day regression beside L55's date-label clearance cases; held by L55, L57, L34 and L45 while each runs)
  - `docs/architecture/publishing/console-charts.md` (the agreement chart's date-label placement rule, updated with Jony's settled choice; also held by L55 and L56 while each runs)
  - Read only: `frontend/src/lib/charts/frame.ts` (`labelWidth`, `AXIS_LABEL_PX` and the shared plot frame). Do not change shared geometry in this row or copy its machinery into the chart.
- **Acceptance gates:** local: inspect `npm --prefix frontend run test:changed -- --list`, run `npm --prefix frontend run test:changed -- --spec console-window.spec.ts` for the regression and existing chart cases, and run `npm --prefix frontend run check`; run `doc_load.py` before and after changing the owning page. Complete integrated browser smoke on Judgement and one other console route; inspect the rendered labels at 390px and 1280px viewport widths in both themes, including empty and all-unread windows. Jony chooses placement; Susan reviews ship sufficiency, including the generated worst case. CI: follow the exact PR head's selected console checks; every group runs on the merge push. Record inputs, commands, results and counts outside the repository; do not repeat unchanged worker checks.
- **Oracle:** In the existing window spec, generate bounded days that put a measured day with a dropped axis tick beside an unread day near the right edge, and put the higher dashed mark near `box.top` so L55's top clamp is triggered after its clearance step. Assert that the actual rendered date-label and mark-label bounding boxes do not intersect, and that the date stays readable inside the plot, at 390px and 1280px in both themes. Keep the existing non-top clearance case and all-unread behavior unchanged. Before dispatch, verify that this new check fails on a separate copy of the exact landed L55 commit for the label overlap, with that copy's code and test settings; absence of `strandedLabels` before L55 is not defect evidence. If the landed code no longer reproduces the overlap, report the result and correct the row before dispatch rather than inventing a failure. Bounding boxes prove separation and containment, not whether the placement preserves the date's association with its day or the mark's meaning; Jony and Susan settle those.

**Decisions**

| # | Decision | Authority |
| --- | --- | --- |
| 1 | This is the unfinished near-top collision L55 discovered, not a new claim that L55's ordinary mark-label clearance failed or that its advisor resolved the corner. L55's existing row and section stay unchanged in this upkeep change | Plan coordinator, 2026-10-09; L55's Follow-ups and decision 8 |
| 2 | L62 waits for L55's landed implementation and L57's edits to the same chart and window spec, and for L34 and L45's edits to the shared window spec. At dispatch compare every named Files touched list against all active rows, including L56's chart and owning-page edits; hold and record any further file dependency before starting | Plan coordinator, 2026-10-09 |
| 3 | Jony selects the smallest local placement that retains the date's link to its day and the dashed mark's meaning. No position, axis change or hidden label is prescribed here. Susan reviews whether the result is sufficient to ship in both themes and widths | To be ruled at execution (Jony and Susan) |
| 4 | Reuse the shared geometry and the chart's existing label-box calculations. If consultation finds that a new surface or shared contract scope is necessary, stop for human scope approval; do not duplicate machinery or take an owner waiver | Plan coordinator, 2026-10-09; section 0 and CLAUDE.md section 6 |
| 5 | Level 1: one chart's local label placement changes; a wrong version is visible and local. No persisted shape moves. The executing change records any durable placement rule in the owning page, and no production code cites this plan | Plan coordinator, 2026-10-09 |

| # | Decision | Authority |

This upkeep change adds only this row and its Reckoner line; it neither closes the defect nor stamps L34's merge | L34's recorded finding; plan coordinator, 2026-10-09 |

L56 currently reads the window spec without changing it, L60 lists it as a gate rather than an edit, L61 lists a different route spec, and L62 edits it; do not invent a dependency from a gate or read-only entry, but hold and record one if an active row's actual edits overlap | Plan coordinator, 2026-10-09; `docs/how-to/execute-a-plan.md` |
| I3 | Read the existing intent before choosing the implementation: source records flatten judged rows first, then unjudged rows, and the current comment names the first source's newest day. Keep judged-row precedence unless the owning rule requires otherwise. Generate a case whose newest judged date is older than its newest unjudged date; investigate what "its newest day" refers to rather than inventing a global newest-source preference. Consult Reader if two defensible meanings remain, record the ruling, and update the owning page only if that rule changes. Susan checks whether the resulting readout is sufficient to ship | Existing source and record-readout rule; Reader resolves a genuine choice at execution, Susan reviews the result |
| I4 | No fix is prescribed: use the smallest structural correction that selects the promised source and day, with existing record identities and helpers where appropriate. Stop for human scope approval if the correction requires shared readout changes, a new surface or a persisted-shape change; section 0 and Table C, C1 remain binding. No coordinator instruction is an owner waiver | Plan coordinator, 2026-10-09; CLAUDE.md sections 0 and 6 |
| I5 | Level 1: the resting index is local to one Voices panel and feeds only its readout; a wrong result is visible there. The shared module is read-only, the emitted data does not change, and no other route consumes this index. Reassess and surface a wider correction if those facts change | Plan author, 2026-10-09, from the named source and its consumer |
